## CHECKPOINT — 2026-08-02, PM (inception run)

Global params: stablecoins −0.62% 7d ($306.7B, shrinking) | MVRV BTC 1.19 / ETH 0.90 | F&G 27 (Fear, Δ7d +1) | funding regime: near-zero across the board (no crowding either way)
Deployed: 0% across 0 positions | Cash: 100% ($10,000 USDT)
Holdings: none
Signals: armed — none (top counts: ADA 5/10, UNI 5/10, BNB 4, CAKE 4, ENA 4, ONDO 4) | confirmed — none | exit-warned — n/a

Decisions & reasoning:
- Inception checkpoint. No coin reaches the 7/10 gate; nothing armed, nothing traded. With p1 (sentiment) unavoidably Neutral this run, the effective ceiling was 6/10 — and no coin cleared even that.
- Regime read: fear (F&G 27) with SHRINKING stablecoin supply and ETH MVRV < 1. Contrarian-constructive on valuation (p9 Bullish), but liquidity (p8) is Bearish — capital is leaving the sidelines pool, not entering. Correct posture is 100% USDT and patience.
- ADA (5/10): +14.9% 7d breakout with RSI 65, golden state, volume z +1.78 on an up day, OI +13% with price. The closest thing to a setup on the board. Needs sentiment + one more parameter to arm.
- UNI (5/10): +28% 30d, RSI 63, OI +19% with price — but 23.5% above its 50DMA, brushing the overextension line (25%). If it arms via further rally, p2 flips Bearish; self-limiting.
- Mechanical label review: no overrides. PUMP p2 correctly Bearish at +30.8% above 50DMA despite uptrend (overextension rule).

Red-team summary: Strongest case against "do nothing": F&G 27 is close to the contrarian-Bullish band (<25) and MVRV says mid-cycle, not top — waiting could miss a fear-bottom bid in ADA/UNI. Rebuttal: the gate exists precisely so that we don't buy 5/10 boards on regime vibes; shrinking stables mean rallies lack fuel. If the bid is real, counts will rise and the 2-checkpoint sequence will catch it a day late — an acceptable cost.

Pre-mortem (PM): scenario "BTC −15% overnight" — book is 100% USDT, damage $0. No de-risk action possible or needed.

Sector exposure: none (all cash)
Watchlist changes: inception build — 10 permanent (BTC, ETH, BNB, XRP, SOL, TRX, DOGE, ZEC, ADA, LINK) + 20 rotating. Sector-cap note: Major-L1 cap (4) fully consumed by permanents ETH/SOL/ADA/TRX → AVAX/SUI/NEAR blocked from rotating slots. Given the entry thesis was explicitly OI-confirmation-dependent and that confirmation has now inverted, holding the full position unchanged would be clinging to a thesis whose named test has failed. Verdict: trim-half stands; the remaining half needs either OI re-expansion or a clean re-break above the 50DMA to restore conviction to Intact, otherwise the next Bearish print or invalidation breach closes it out entirely.

Response: no de-risking action taken — single half-sized position and 95%+ cash is already the primary risk control in place; the position's own short-leash review (above) is the more relevant near-term watch item than a market-wide pre-mortem action tonight.

On MORPHO, p1 is specifically the *social sentiment/crowd behavior* parameter — TVL growth and exchange-outflow stats are on-chain/fundamental data already captured elsewhere in the rubric (p7 OI, p9 MVRV), not something p1 should double-count; "flat interest, no crowd extreme" is a defensible, non-motivated Neutral read even though the narrative color was more positive. A disciplined process should arguably trim the remainder now on the accumulating pattern (3 of 5 checkpoints net-negative 7d OI) rather than wait for a fourth data point. **Rebuttal:** the mechanical p7 rubric threshold (oi_7d < -15% with a rising week) exists precisely so overrides are triggered by rule, not by accumulating discomfort with a metric that is genuinely volatile at TRX's OI scale — forcing a Bearish label (or an ad-hoc trim) on a -3.4% print because it "feels like enough checkpoints now" is the same discretion-overrides-rules failure this system exists to prevent, just aimed at the exit side instead of the entry side. Estimated drawdown: TRX would likely track a meaningful fraction of a sharp BTC selloff given majors correlation, plausibly -10% to -20% on TRX itself, but at half size the portfolio-level impact is modest (roughly -0.5% to -1.0% of total portfolio value in that scenario). Response: no de-risking action taken — a single half-sized position and 95%+ cash is already the primary risk control in place; TRX's own short-leash review (above, tied to the 2026-08-26 interim date) is the more relevant near-term watch item than a market-wide pre-mortem action tonight.

Noted tension I chose not to resolve by force: 24h OI +57.9% / 7d OI +81.5% on the highest-beta meme in the book is a violent leverage build that *feels* like crowded longs — but funding is 0.01%/8h (flat), and the rubric's p7 Bearish conditions are "OI rising + price falling" or "OI collapsing after a rally", neither of which holds. Kept the mechanical Bullish and let the euphoria read land where it belongs, in p1. This is the arm I trust least.
- **FIL (7/10, 0 Bearish, armed).** The sharpest reversal on the board — 3/10 with 3 Bearish this morning to 7/10 with none tonight, on a single +11.1% day that took price back above the 50DMA ($0.7309) and flipped p2 Bearish→Neutral, p3 to Bullish (RSI 58.4), p5 to Bullish (z +4.41). Price remains well below the 200DMA ($0.8678), so this is a bounce inside a downtrend, not a repaired structure. A 12-hour, four-parameter swing is exactly the kind of print that should be made to prove itself over a second checkpoint before any capital moves — which is what the gate does.
- **Notable non-arms.** XRP 6/10 (RSI 75.9 Bearish caps it; p1 downgraded Bearish→Neutral as yesterday's viral unverified "FedNow" claim washed out of the 48h window). SOL 6/10 (RSI 77.3; p1 Bullish→Neutral, the crowd fear that justified the AM read has turned into pump-chasing). ETH 6/10 with 2 Bearish (RSI 84.2, the most overbought print on the board, plus euphoric p1). LINK 5/10 with **3 Bearish** — +25.7% above its 50DMA, RSI 81.2, and rising-interest euphoria on the White House summit catalyst; the clearest "do not touch" on the board tonight.
- **Parameter-1 discipline.** All 28 coins at mechanical ≥5 were run through `scripts/sentiment.py` (two batches, 17 + 11, both exit 0, real citations). ONDO and ASTER at mechanical 4/9 were not run and are Neutral by the Section 0 amendment, not fabricated. Five p1 labels moved off Neutral: **Bearish** on ETH, LINK, ENA, ZEC and PEPE — every one of them a rising-interest-or-euphoria print on a name that has already run hard. **Zero p1 labels were set Bullish.** I want that on the record: with three coins sitting one label short of a higher count and two more one label short of arming, the temptation to find a Bullish sentiment read was structural, and the honest data did not support a single one. XLM and FIL were each explicitly considered for a contrarian-Bullish flip and each declined in SIGNALS.csv with reasoning.
- **Override register (one, mechanical, non-decisional).** **SHIB p2 Bearish → Neutral.** `PARAMETERS.json` stores `dma50`/`dma200` rounded to one significant figure for sub-$0.00001 tokens — both serialise as `5e-06` — so SHIB's price of 4.91e-06 evaluated as *below both* moving averages and tripped the Bearish branch. Recomputed from raw Binance daily closes: true 50DMA **4.5016e-06**, true 200DMA **5.4249e-06**. Price is **+9.1% above** the 50DMA and below the 200DMA with the death cross intact — the script's own Neutral branch. Data-precision artifact, not a market signal. SHIB goes 5B/1x → 6B/0x, which does not change its gate status. **Carried to LESSONS.md as a tooling hypothesis** — the same rounding applies to PEPE (checked: true 50DMA 2.7706e-06, price above it, label Neutral either way, so PEPE was not affected tonight) and will silently misprice p2 for any micro-priced token in future runs.
- **Data quality:** no outages. `parameters.py` completed for all 30 coins + global block; `sentiment.py` completed for all 28 requested coins. The only standing degradation is the known Binance-futures geo-block, handled by the sanctioned Hyperliquid fallback (`futures_source: hyperliquid` recorded on all 30). Two unverified X claims surfaced and were logged but **not** treated as Section 2 auto-excludes, since neither has any corroborating report: a "potential infinite supply hack in 2026" rumour on **ZEC**, and a fake-airdrop phishing link circulating at **VIRTUAL** holders (a scam targeting holders, not a protocol compromise). Both flagged for the next MID red-flag sweep.

Red-team summary: **The strongest case against tonight's inaction** is that this is the best board the system has printed in a week — three coins over the gate, one of them at 8/10 in a clean golden state — arriving on a day when stablecoin supply is growing, MVRV is healthy and 27 of 30 coins are green. A system that watches a broad risk-on impulse from 100% cash and does nothing may be mistaking rule-following for judgment, and the two-checkpoint gate guarantees it buys a day later and higher than the move it identified. **Rebuttal:** the gate exists precisely for a tape like this one. Look at what actually generated the arms — p4, p5 and p7 all flip Bullish mechanically on any violent up-day (vol expansion on upside, volume z on an up day, OI building with price), so a single impulsive session manufactures three "new" Bullish labels across the entire watchlist at once. FIL's 3/10 → 7/10 in twelve hours is the proof: that is the tape moving, not FIL's setup improving. The second checkpoint is the only thing distinguishing a real setup from a one-day squeeze, and this book has already paid for skipping that lesson — four staged-entry halves (CAKE/ETH/ZEC/MORPHO) were cut yesterday for exactly this failure mode. **The counter-case that does concern me** is the other direction: three simultaneous arms into an F&G that has sprinted +33 points means tomorrow's AM could hand me multiple confirmed candidates at once, at the top of the move, with sector caps and Section 8 floors as the only brakes. Pre-committing now, while nothing is at stake: if XLM/PEPE/FIL confirm together, XLM takes priority (highest count, +0.8% extension, cleanest structure), PEPE is the one I am prepared to decline outright despite a qualifying count (euphoric p1, +81.5% 7d OI build, meme beta into a greed spike), and no more than two new positions open in a single checkpoint regardless of how many confirm. **Third angle — am I hiding behind the rulebook on p1?** Five Bearish and zero Bullish sentiment calls is a directionally lopsided set on a day the market ripped. Checked: the lopsidedness is data-driven, not tonal — every Bearish call cites explicit euphoria markers with rising interest, and the Neutral calls on XLM/FIL/BTC/DOGE each note the constructive absence-of-euphoria reading and explain why "flat interest" fails the rubric's *improving* interest test. The rubric's Bullish branch is genuinely narrow. Flagged for the monthly review as a possible p1 asymmetry: if p1 can only realistically print Bearish or Neutral in a rising tape, it is a one-directional parameter and should not be counted as a full tenth of the gate.

Pre-mortem (PM): **Scenario — BTC −15% overnight.** With zero positions the direct portfolio drawdown is **$0.00 (0.0%)**; the de-risk trigger (one scenario plausibly hitting ≥3 positions hard) cannot fire against an empty book, and cash is already the maximum defensive state. The honest pre-mortem tonight is therefore about tomorrow, not today: all three armed coins are high-beta to BTC (XLM Payments, PEPE meme, FIL DePIN — historical BTC correlation is the default assumption), so a −15% BTC night would break every one of their p2/p3/p5/p7 labels simultaneously and the arms would lapse at AM without a trade ever being placed — the gate self-protects here. **The scenario that actually costs this book money is the mirror image:** the melt-up continues one more session, all three confirm at AM into F&G ~70+, capital deploys near the high, and the reversal arrives afterward. **Response (pre-committed above):** maximum two new positions at any single checkpoint; XLM prioritised on extension and structure; PEPE declinable on a qualifying count; Section 8 floors (R ≥ 2.0, EV > 0) applied strictly with no rounding-up of p, and staged entry at half size as always. No action required tonight.

Sector exposure: none — 100% cash, 0 open positions. (Armed names sit in three distinct sectors — XLM Payments, PEPE Meme, FIL DePIN/Storage — so hard rule 8 would not bind even if all three confirmed.)
Watchlist changes (MID only): n/a — PM checkpoint. Composition unchanged from the 2026-08-20 MID run (no adds/drops; sector caps and the 4/5 slot-change budget both blocking). Excluded (red flags): none new on watchlist tickers; two unverified rumours logged above (ZEC, VIRTUAL) and carried to the next MID sweep.
Simulated fills: **n/a — no trades.** No coin reached a second consecutive qualifying checkpoint.
Learning artifacts written: `state/SIGNALS.csv` (30 rows, 2026-08-20 PM, full 10-label set + per-coin p1 reasoning + the SHIB override rationale), `state/PARAMETERS.json` (refreshed 30 coins + global, plus X-sentiment blocks with citations for 28 coins), `state/LESSONS.md` (two new falsifiable hypotheses: the micro-price DMA rounding defect, and the p1 one-directionality question), `state/CHECKPOINT_LOG.md` (this report), `state/BRIEFING.md`. No entry snapshot or exit post-mortem due — no position opened or closed. Shadow book untouched: next refresh is Monday 2026-08-24 AM, and no confirmed candidate has been rejected (three lapsed/pending arms do not qualify under Section 11.2).

No basis to override the rule in either direction.

No basis to withhold the trade the rules confirmed; conservatism was applied through sizing and p, not through overriding a clean signal. **Second angle — is the broad market overheating itself grounds to stand down entirely?** Nearly every other major (AAVE through ZEC) mechanically fails on p3/p6 this checkpoint precisely because of the overheating — UNI and XLM are the two names that structurally *didn't* fail those tests, which is closer to "correctly excluding the froth" than "riding the froth." **Red-team summary:**
- **Against nudging AAVE's stated p from 0.38 to 0.40 to clear the Tier C floor** — is this the "forcing a 7th parameter to justify a trade you already want" failure mode Section 7 warns against? **Rebuttal:** the confluence count itself (7/10) was already met independently for 3 consecutive checkpoints, unaffected by this adjustment — p only feeds sizing/EV, not the entry gate. The nudge is grounded in a real, measured technical change (dev-from-50DMA eased -4.5pp as price pulled back) rather than an invented justification, and the standing risk factor (overextension) is not waved away — it's still capped at the floor, not raised further, and explicitly flagged here rather than quietly assumed.
- **Against cutting JUP and ASTER given LESSONS.md's extensive, dollar-quantified history of costly staged-entry false negatives (hypotheses #2, #7, #9, #10 — opportunity cost already >$1,100 on six prior cuts this month)** — should this checkpoint's cuts be held instead, given that history? **Rebuttal:** the documented false-negative pattern is specifically about *mechanical sub-indicator* rollovers (RSI/rvol, funding/F&G) firing while price and OI — the parameters actually named in each entry's thesis test — held or improved. Both of this checkpoint's cuts are driven entirely by p1 sentiment, a judgment-call parameter explicitly designed to swing checkpoint-to-checkpoint (see hypothesis #8), not a sub-indicator rollover. Overriding the plainly-stated staged-entry rule here — without a monthly-review-sanctioned amendment and explicit user sign-off — would substitute discretion for the rule the system exists to enforce ("rules always win over your enthusiasm for a trade"). Flagging as a genuinely close call given the dollar history, but not overriding.
- **Book is now 4 altcoin positions (~34% deployed), all correlated to BTC, inside a still-hot melt-up (BTC RSI 79.4)** — addressed in the pre-mortem below rather than by not entering; the confluence gate, not discretionary risk appetite, determines deployment, and AAVE cleared it on 3 consecutive checkpoints.

**Pre-mortem:** **Scenario — "BTC –15% overnight."** BTC sits at RSI 79.4, dev-from-50DMA +18.6% (cooled slightly from PM 08-25/AM 08-26's hotter reads but still firmly in melt-up territory), F&G 65 (Δ7d +19, greed regime, though also cooled from the 71-74 range seen mid-week) — a sharp BTC-led reversal remains the standing base-case tail risk, as every prior pre-mortem has flagged. **Estimated drawdown:** the book now holds 4 positions (XLM, ONDO, MORPHO, AAVE), all altcoins with historical beta >1 to BTC in drawdowns — deployed capital is ~34.25% ($3,383 of $9,874). A plausible -20% to -30% mark-to-market hit on the deployed sleeve is roughly -$680 to -$1,015, or -6.9% to -10.3% of total portfolio value; cash (65.75%) is entirely insulated. **Does this plausibly hit ≥3 positions hard?** Yes — for the first time this book has 4 positions, all correlated altcoin exposure with no BTC/cash hedge beyond the cash buffer itself; this explicitly meets the Section 5 step 11 trigger. **Response:** no forced de-risking trade this checkpoint — cash remains the dominant position (65.75%) and is functioning as the diversifier of last resort per Section 9, and each position is still staged/half-or-newly-sized (AAVE is a fresh half; MORPHO just reached full size). But this is the first checkpoint where the ≥3-positions pre-mortem trigger is genuinely live rather than hypothetical — flagging explicitly for the next checkpoint: if BTC's RSI pushes materially higher (toward the 82+ readings seen mid-week) without a corresponding pullback, or if a 5th position enters, the book should weigh trimming rather than adding further altcoin beta into the same regime risk.

**Red-team summary:**
- **Against the AAVE sector-cap decision** — is sizing down a confirmed staged-entry add really different from overriding the confluence gate itself, the exact failure mode Section 9 warns against? **Rebuttal:** the confluence/staged-entry rule and the sector-concentration rule are independent hard constraints — one governs entry timing/confirmation, the other governs portfolio-level concentration regardless of any single trade's merit. AAVE's thesis, confluence, and staged-entry confirmation are all honored in full; only the *size* of the add was constrained, by a different rule that would have been breached by the standard mechanical sizing. Cutting the add to zero would have been the wrong response (it would treat a sector-cap conflict as a thesis failure it isn't); executing the full add would have silently broken a rule stated as "never break, no exceptions." Sizing to the compliant maximum threads this correctly.
- **Against XRP's p1 read** — XRP is a permanent watchlist slot with a standing "rangebound" thesis; is reading capitulation into ordinary holder grumbling a stretch? **Rebuttal:** the sentiment data shows a genuine narrative shift flagged by the script itself ("shift from typical hype to visible impatience and skepticism"), zero euphoria markers, and falling interest — this is a materially different read from XRP's usual flat/mixed checkpoints, not a forced fit. The confluence gate (7/10) still requires a second consecutive confirming checkpoint before anything is committed.
- **Book concentration** — DeFi Lending now sits at essentially the sector cap (49.99% of deployed capital) by design after today's cap enforcement; if MORPHO or AAVE extends further while the other holds, the *next* add or rebalance in that sector will likely be blocked outright rather than partially capped. Flagged for the next checkpoint, not actionable today.

Rather than a discretionary preemptive trim of four Playing-Out/Intact positions (which would itself violate the rules-over-enthusiasm discipline), the response is to (a) hold the existing cash buffer rather than chase the 5th slot into more BTC-beta exposure today, letting TRX/POL sit through a full second confirming checkpoint before either is considered, and (b) rely on each position's hard invalidation price as the automatic, non-discretionary circuit breaker if the scenario fires. No forced action today.

Treating a same-day, same-direction sentiment normalization across multiple coins as an actionable signal — rather than checking each position's actual Bearish count and invalidation headroom — would be exactly the kind of correlated, narrative-driven overreaction the rules are built to filter out.
- **Against reading AAVE's sentiment flip (Bullish→Bearish) as noise** — could this be an early sign the "quietly exiting" capitulation theme that has supported AAVE all hold is genuinely over, with real distribution starting? **Rebuttal:** possible, and explicitly flagged as a fresh watch item above — but one checkpoint's absence of a recurring theme isn't evidence of reversal on its own, especially with price still +0.71% vs avg entry and OI still confirming (7d +24.3%). If euphoria persists next checkpoint (i.e., the capitulation theme stays absent) alongside continued overextension, that combination — not this single data point — is the actionable signal.
- **Against POL's Neutral p1 read costing the book a live entry** — is being this conservative on a mixed sentiment read the right call when the book is sitting on ~61% cash with one slot open? **Rebuttal:** yes — Section 9's core warning is precisely against rounding an ambiguous parameter toward the trade you already have a reason to want (an open slot). POL's own sentiment data this run shows falling interest and genuine two-sided chatter (dump warnings alongside price targets), a materially different and more balanced picture than PM 08-27's clean capitulation-only read that first armed it. If POL's setup is real, it will likely re-arm on a subsequent checkpoint with a cleaner read.

- *Against the ONDO trim being reactive to one bad day rather than a real signal* — could the 50DMA breach and OI reversal both just be beta, not ONDO-specific weakness, making this trim premature? **Rebuttal:** plausible and explicitly logged as such in JOURNAL.md — but the trim was sized conservatively (half, not a full exit) precisely to hedge that uncertainty, and the trigger was the entry thesis's *own named test condition* failing, not a discretionary overreaction to price alone. - *Against the AAVE sector-cap trim being arbitrary* (why AAVE and not MORPHO?) — is this correction defensible or just a convenient excuse to trim the position with better current stats? **Rebuttal:** the choice logic (lower confluence count, already-precedented as the position constrained by this rule at entry) was fixed *before* looking at which trim would "feel" better, and is stated explicitly as a compliance action, not a thesis judgment — AAVE's underlying structure is acknowledged as the strongest of the four holdings in the same paragraph. - *Against not de-risking further given today's correlated hit to all 4 holdings* — should more than two trims have happened? **Rebuttal:** cash is now 71.39% of the book (up from 61.3% this morning) purely from today's two trims — an already very large defensive buffer for a 4-position, ~28.6%-deployed book. No holding has breached invalidation or hit the exit gate. Further blanket trimming without a position-specific trigger would itself be the action-bias failure mode Section 9 names.

Corrected using the same lowest-confluence-first logic established 2026-08-28 PM: AAVE (6/10) absorbed first, but zeroing it alone was insufficient (would still land at ~67.3% with MORPHO's full-target size undisturbed), so MORPHO (7/10, the book's strongest read) also absorbed a trim down to bring the sector to ~49% of deployed capital. Both AAVE's exit and MORPHO's trim are explicitly compliance-driven, not thesis downgrades — logged in full in JOURNAL.md and flagged as an escalation of LESSONS.md hypothesis #11.

**Red-team summary:**
- **Against combining the XLM trim and ONDO exit in the same checkpoint, given the sector-cap cascade it triggered** — would staggering these two decisions across checkpoints have avoided gutting DeFi Lending? **Rebuttal:** each call stands independently on its own named-thesis-test evidence (XLM's DMA/RSI compression, ONDO's fired exit-condition) and delaying either to dodge a downstream compliance consequence would be worse discipline than executing the risk call and correcting compliance mechanically — exactly the pattern already established 2026-08-28 PM. Holding a deteriorating position specifically to avoid triggering a portfolio-construction rule is not a defensible reason to hold it.
- **Against trusting the XLM 7d OI print (-14.3%, a swing from PM 08-28's +52.2%)** — is this real deterioration or a rolling-window artifact of the self-built OI_HISTORY.json? **Rebuttal:** flagged explicitly in the trim reasoning and JOURNAL.md as a data-quality caveat, weighted as corroborating rather than the primary trigger — the trim's actual basis (dev-from-50DMA at the thinnest cushion of the hold, RSI exiting its band for a second checkpoint) rests on the more robust, non-OI_HISTORY-dependent price/DMA data.
- **Against forcing a trim on MORPHO, the single strongest read in the book this checkpoint (7/10, 0 Bearish, improving sentiment)** — does mechanically resizing the book's best thesis to satisfy a portfolio-math rule make sense? **Rebuttal:** no thesis judgment changed (Playing Out, Intact, logged explicitly as compliance-only in both PORTFOLIO.json and JOURNAL.md) — but this is flagged as a genuine structural tension in the rulebook, not waved away: with only 2-3 positions typically held, any 2-position sector is fragile to this cap whenever an unrelated position shrinks. Logged as an escalation of LESSONS.md #11 with a proposed sharpening (consider suspending the 50%-of-deployed-capital rule, not the max-2-positions-per-sector rule, when the book is running 3 or fewer total positions) for the 2026-09-01 monthly review.
- **Against not de-risking further given today's book-wide reduction already** — is a 90.6%-cash, 2-position book now too conservative? **Rebuttal:** no — Section 9 explicitly treats cash as always a valid state and the diversifier of last resort; both remaining positions (XLM half, MORPHO trimmed) still have Intact-to-Weakening theses with no invalidation breach, and the book remains open to fresh entries at the next qualifying confluence signal.
